The phase-1 tasklist explicitly names this module as the destination (\"`src/superclaude/cli/eval/models.py` with the 8 fields from DM-005\"), and T01.15's `Optional[\"ExpectFailure\"]` forward annotation was authored on the assumption the concrete class would land in the same module. Co-locating the pair removes the forward reference at evaluation time without needing a `TYPE_CHECKING` dance.\n\n2. **`expected` / `actual` typed as `Any`.** DM-005 does not constrain payload shape, and primitive Expects (COMP-010.1–6, M4) will populate these with arbitrary JSON-shaped values (int exit codes, regex strings, dict event payloads). Narrower typing now would force a refactor when primitives land. `Any` matches the DM-005 spirit (\"assertion failure detail\") and keeps the contract permissive.\n\n3. **`message` defaults to `\"\"`.** Symmetric with `ExpectResult.message` from T01.15 — callers should be able to omit the message when the `expected`/`actual` diff is self-explanatory. The Reporter renders the empty string as \"no summary message\" rather than treating absence as an error.\n\n4. **`artifact_ref` and `traceback` typed as `Optional[str]` with default `None`.** DM-005 does not state these are required. The Reporter generates artifact refs as part of writing the JSON report (post-construction), so the dataclass must accept the missing case at construction time. Same reasoning for `traceback`: only the exception path captures it.\n\n5. **`to_dict()` builds the dict explicitly from `_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS`.** Chose this over `dataclasses.asdict(self)` to guarantee ordering. `asdict` returns insertion order which currently matches field-declaration order, but the spec demands a stable contract for reporter snapshots, and an explicit field tuple makes the guarantee visible. The test `test_expect_failure_to_dict_field_order_matches_dm005` locks this. `test_expect_failure_asdict_matches_to_dict` documents that the two agree on values today (ordering is owned by `to_dict`).\n\n6. **No `__post_init__` validation.** DM-005 does not require value-level checks (unlike DM-007 `Capability.failure_mode` from T01.09 which enumerated literals). Adding validation would risk false negatives — the Reporter is the right layer to decide what \"looks empty\" means for an `expected` payload of, say, `None` or `0`.\n\n7. **Forward reference resolution for `ExpectResult.failure`.** T01.15 already accommodates an `Optional[\"ExpectFailure\"]` forward annotation via `from __future__ import annotations`. T01.16 lands the concrete class in the same module, so the annotation now resolves to a real type at runtime. No production-code change is needed in `ExpectResult`; the test stand-in from T01.15 is superseded by `test_expect_failure_round_trips_inside_expect_result` here, which exercises the genuine `ExpectFailure` inside an `ExpectResult`.\n\n8. **Package re-export.** Added `ExpectFailure` to `src/superclaude/cli/eval/__init__.py`'s `__all__` so callers can `from superclaude.cli.eval import ExpectFailure` — symmetric with `ExpectResult` and `EvalSpec`. Locked by `test_expect_failure_importable_from_package`.\n\n9. **Module docstring updated.** Listed DM-005 / D-0014 / T01.16 alongside DM-002 / DM-009 and described the 8-field ExpectFailure contract so the file's purpose statement stays accurate.\n\n## Things deliberately NOT in scope of T01.16\n\n- Real `ExpectCallable` primitives that emit populated `ExpectFailure`s — M4 (COMP-010.1–6).\n- Reporter side-effects: artifact ref resolution against the run's scratch root, JSONL writes, terminal rendering — COMP-008 / T03.13.\n- `EvalOutcome` aggregation of multiple `ExpectFailure`s per eval — T03.01.\n- A \"two failing Expects in a single eval → two ExpectFailure entries\" *integration* test — that lives in the Reporter test set / T03.13. T01.16 covers the per-failure construction pattern (`test_expect_failure_two_per_eval_pattern`).\n\n## Risks observed during build\n\n- **`Any` typing on `expected` / `actual`.** Permissive by design, but a future strict-typing pass may want a generic narrowing once primitive payload shapes are known (M4). The dataclass is frozen, so any tightening will be a non-breaking annotation-only change.\n- **`to_dict()` ordering vs. asdict().** Two serialisation paths exist (`to_dict()` and `dataclasses.asdict`). They agree on values today; only `to_dict()` guarantees ordering. The Reporter (T03.13) must call `to_dict()` — `asdict` is acceptable for ad-hoc tests but not for snapshot diffs. The spec.md *Serialisation* section documents this.\n---EVIDENCE---\ncat: '.dev/releases/current/cliEval/evidence/T01.16/*.md': No such file or directory","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"342c4995-d7b0-4198-b9e2-eff5fced7b76","uuid":"3c12574b-fb36-4591-befe-f0df730b0d54","timestamp":"2026-05-20T06:19:45.155Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"# D-0014 — implementation notes\n\n## Decisions made during build\n\n1. **Module location.** Added `ExpectFailure` to `src/superclaude/cli/eval/models.py` alongside `EvalSpec` and `ExpectResult`. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01Di5uRTpRpfff5bzR5Ezd6U","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01YD7A1kSwRLJ19G9teCacko","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0015/notes.md","content":"# D-0015 — implementation notes\n\n## Decisions made during build\n\n1. **Why `uv pip list` instead of parsing `pyproject.toml`.** AC3 names\n   `pexpect` explicitly even though it is purely transitive (via vendored\n   `ptytest`). Parsing `[project.dependencies]` would never see `pexpect`\n   because it is not a direct dep, so the check would silently pass even if\n   ptytest brought in pexpect ≥ N versions. `uv pip list` captures the\n   actual installed surface, which is what AC3 is constraining.\n\n2. **Allow-list as a committed file (`scripts/dependency_baseline.txt`)\n   rather than a Python constant.** The list moves through PR review when a\n   real dep change happens. Putting it in a Python constant would conflate\n   \"policy data\" with \"policy logic\" — the spec wants the data to be the\n   diffable artefact.\n\n3. **Name-only comparison (versions ignored).** Pinning versions in the\n   baseline would mean every minor `pytest` bump fails CI, which is noise.\n   AC3 is a *boundary* check, not a *lockfile*; the dependency-lock concern\n   is a separate, future-roadmap item.\n\n4. **PEP 503 normalisation.** Both `uv pip list` and source-of-truth lists\n   can drift on `_` vs `-` and casing. Normalising (`lower()` +\n   `replace('_','-')`) makes the check stable across upstream metadata\n   renames.\n\n5. **Removals do not fail.** Allow-list is treated as an upper bound, not an\n   equality check. A dev tool can be dropped without ceremony; only\n   additions are the AC3 concern.\n\n6. **`ptyprocess` pre-added to allow-list.** `pexpect` requires `ptyprocess`\n   at runtime, so AC3's explicit `pexpect` allowance implicitly carries\n   `ptyprocess`. Pre-adding it avoids a CI surprise when the ptytest vendor\n   drop lands in Phase 2.\n\n7. **`superclaude` itself in the baseline.** The editable install registers\n   `superclaude` as a top-level package, so it appears in `uv pip list`. It\n   stays in the baseline so the check passes today.\n\n8. **`jsonschema` not separately re-listed in the AC3-additions block.** It\n   is already a direct dep in `pyproject.toml` and thus in the pre-eval-CLI\n   baseline snapshot. AC3's mention of `jsonschema` is to clarify that its\n   retention is approved, not that it is a new addition.\n\n9. **Separate CI job rather than a step in `test`.** Keeps the green/red\n   signal isolated so a dep-boundary breach reads as such in the run page,\n   not as \"tests failed\". The job is added to `test-summary`'s `needs:`\n   list so the umbrella status reflects it.\n\n10. **No matrix for the CI job.** The Python-version axis is not relevant\n    to the allow-list comparison — the installed set is determined by\n    `pyproject.toml`, not the interpreter version. Running once on 3.10\n    matches the `lint` job's pattern.\n\n11. **Exit code 2 reserved for tooling errors.** `uv` missing or baseline\n    file missing returns 2 (matches the FR-SCH2 convention used by the\n    eval CLI loader). Exit 1 is the actual AC3 violation. Exit 0 is the\n    happy path.\n\n## Things deliberately NOT in scope of T01.17\n\n- A `uv.lock` / pinned lockfile gate — AC3 is name-only.\n- Detecting removed dev tools — not an AC3 concern.\n- Auto-updating the baseline — that would defeat the PR-review purpose.\n- Wiring `pexpect` into `pyproject.toml` — Phase 2 (ptytest vendor) owns it.\n- Enforcing the allow-list at `uv pip install` time (pre-install hook) — CI\n  gate is sufficient per the EXEMPT tier classification (Section 5.3).\n\n## Risks observed during build\n\n- **Baseline drift after upstream wheels rename a package.** PEP 503\n  normalisation handles the common case (`_` ↔ `-`, casing), but a wholesale\n  rename (e.g., `pyyaml` → `yaml`) would fail until the baseline is updated.\n  This is a feature: such renames should be reviewed, not silently absorbed.\n\n- **`uv` not on PATH in some dev environments.** Exit 2 with a clear error\n  message means the dev sees the cause; CI installs `uv` explicitly via the\n  existing UV install step.\n\n- **Editable installs of sibling projects** (e.g., a contributor running\n  `uv pip install -e ../neighbour` into the venv) would inject packages\n  that fail the check. This is the intended behaviour — `make verify-deps`\n  represents the *committed* dependency surface, not a contributor's local\n  experimentation.\n\n## CI behaviour summary\n\n```\ntest-summary\n├── test          (matrix: 3.10, 3.11, 3.12)\n├── lint\n├── plugin-check\n├── doctor-check\n└── verify-deps   ← new (single Python 3.10 run)\n```\n\nAll five must pass for `test-summary` to be green.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2432,"cache_read_input_tokens":128901,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2432,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":40,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"342c4995-d7b0-4198-b9e2-eff5fced7b76","uuid":"947b3aa3-9cab-41f6-a846-88bed4fca2ae"}
